## 175 Goldbach's conjecture

**Conditions prior.** The statement holds across readings. The terms have determinate reference. The accounts hold their truth value while tested. A procedure repeated returns the same outcome independently of what precedes it. Nothing prior enters.

**Statement.** Is every even number greater than two the sum of two primes?

**The field's account of its hardness.** That sieve theory was invented to count prime patterns and has proved able to give good upper bounds while remaining largely unable to lower-bound the primes themselves. The obstruction is stated in the field's own terms: where a set's elements are all products of an odd number of primes, or all products of an even number, sieve methods without additional ingredients give no non-trivial lower bound on its size, and any upper bound is off by a factor of two or more. The principle's originator described sets of integers as tending to be evenly distributed with respect to the parity of their number of prime factors unless constructed with a built-in bias. The field records that even reproving the infinitude of the primes by sieve alone is extremely difficult, and that the obstruction persists under strong distributional conjectures.

**Accounts.** Every such number is a sum of two primes: verified past 4 × 10¹⁸, heuristic density arguments predict the number of representations grows rather than shrinks, and near-results are strong — every sufficiently large odd number is a sum of three primes, proved unconditionally.

**Specifications.** The additive and multiplicative structures of the integers relate in a way a proof could exhibit — local.

**Specifications withdrawn.**

**Common requirement.** That the statement is true or false of all even numbers.

**Conserving relation.** A proof holding for every even number greater than two.

**Conserving reach.** All even numbers greater than two.

**Reach.** The statement quantifies over all even numbers. Verification to a bound, and asymptotic methods with stated limits, are what is measured. The two are not the same.

**Unreachability.** The circle method proves the ternary case and the binary case lies beyond its reach for reasons the method itself makes precise; and the parity obstruction in sieve theory is a known and stated block to reaching two primes, the last step not being a matter of refinement. The proof is not reached over the even numbers by computation, the circle method, or sieve methods.

**Persistence.** A proof, or a counterexample.

**Addresses.** Hardness, and the statement of the obstruction: Tao, "Open question: the parity problem in sieve theory", 2007, and the parity-problem summary at Wikipedia, "Parity problem (sieve theory)". Selberg's own formulation and the 1949 naming: Selberg, as quoted in "Twin primes and the parity problem", Journal of Number Theory, 2017, §1. The sieve result as the exhibition of the barrier, and bilinear-form parity breaking: arXiv:math/0507569, §1. The bounded-error obstruction argument and its stated escapes: Tao, "The parity problem obstruction for the binary Goldbach problem with bounded error", 2014. Parity-sensitive sieves: the Wikipedia summary, citing Friedlander and Iwaniec from 1996. Remaining properties: keyed at the next pass.

*The report.*

**Approaches.** Computation — verified past 4 × 10¹⁸, returning more of the same domain. The circle method — proves the ternary case by estimating the relevant exponential sums, the binary case beyond its reach for reasons the method makes precise. Sieve methods — every sufficiently large even number a prime plus a number with at most two prime factors, recorded as the quintessential exhibition of the parity barrier, sieve methods counting numbers with few prime factors more readily than numbers with exactly one. Bilinear form estimates — the ternary resolution introduced the idea that estimating certain bilinear forms can break the barrier, with further instances recorded since; the binary case is not among them. Parity-sensitive sieves — developed from the late 1990s and recorded as making the obstruction less of an obstacle, without removing it here. Bounded-error variants — an argument recorded that the obstruction rules out modifications of the bounded-gaps method for the binary case, with the stated escapes being a failure of the underlying randomness principle or control of combinations of more than one multiplicative function. Density and heuristic arguments — predict many representations, not proofs, and known to be unable to become proofs by refinement. Assuming the Riemann hypothesis — improves bounds and does not close the binary case.

**Narrowing recorded.** The ternary result narrows the reach: the sum-of-three statement stands proved for sufficiently large odd numbers. Sieve results narrow the relation: a prime plus a near-prime stands proved in place of two primes.

**Progress.** The verification to 4 × 10¹⁸. The unconditional ternary theorem. The sieve result and the stated parity obstruction.

**Own record.**

## 176 Twin primes

**Conditions prior.** The statement holds across readings. The terms have determinate reference. The accounts hold their truth value while tested. A procedure repeated returns the same outcome independently of what precedes it. Nothing prior enters.

**Statement.** Are there infinitely many pairs of primes differing by two?

**The field's account of its hardness.** That sieve theory was invented to count prime patterns and has proved able to give good upper bounds while remaining largely unable to lower-bound the primes themselves. The obstruction is stated in the field's own terms: where a set's elements are all products of an odd number of primes, or all products of an even number, sieve methods without additional ingredients give no non-trivial lower bound on its size, and any upper bound is off by a factor of two or more. The principle's originator described sets of integers as tending to be evenly distributed with respect to the parity of their number of prime factors unless constructed with a built-in bias. The field records that even reproving the infinitude of the primes by sieve alone is extremely difficult, and that the obstruction persists under strong distributional conjectures.

**Accounts.** There are infinitely many: an explicit asymptotic density for twin pairs is conjectured from a heuristic in which primes behave randomly subject to their known congruence constraints, and it matches computation closely over every range checked. The same heuristic predicts densities for other prime constellations, and those predictions have matched wherever they can be tested.

**Specifications.** Prime distribution is governed by a density — local. A density argument can establish infinitude of a specific gap — local.

**Specifications withdrawn.**

**Common requirement.** That the statement is true or false of the integers.

**Conserving relation.** A proof of infinitude for the gap of two.

**Conserving reach.** All primes.

**Reach.** The statement quantifies over all primes. Sieve methods with a proved obstruction, and computation to a bound, are what is measured. The two are not the same.

**Unreachability.** The parity obstruction is proved: sieve methods cannot distinguish numbers with an even number of prime factors from an odd number, so the reduction from the bounded gap to two is blocked by parity rather than by effort. The proof is not reached over the primes by sieve methods or computation.

**Persistence.** A proof of infinitude for the gap of two, or a proof that the pairs are finite.

**Addresses.** Hardness, and the statement of the obstruction: Tao, "Open question: the parity problem in sieve theory", 2007. Selberg's formulation and the naming: "Twin primes and the parity problem", Journal of Number Theory, 2017, §1. The bound of six under the generalised conjecture, and the argument that no sieve-theoretic method does better: Polymath, "Variants of the Selberg sieve, and bounded intervals containing many primes", arXiv:1407.4897, §8. The closest classical approximation as an exhibition of the barrier: arXiv:math/0507569, §1. The conditional route through irregular zeros, and the constant-multiple evasion: Tao, "The parity problem obstruction for the binary Goldbach problem with bounded error", 2014, and remark 1 at the same author's parity-problem notes. Remaining properties: keyed at the next pass.

*The report.*

**Approaches.** Computation — very large twin pairs found, with no bearing on infinitude. Sieve methods — infinitely many primes whose successor at distance two is prime or a product of two primes, recorded as the closest classical approximation and as the exhibition of the barrier. Bounded gaps — successive results reaching a gap of 246, with the field's own analysis arguing that the barrier prohibits sieve-theoretic methods, including those used, from any bound stronger than six even under the generalised distributional conjecture and even using sieves other than the one named. Parity-sensitive sieves — developed from the late 1990s, recorded as making the obstruction less of an obstacle, without reaching two. Related-multiplicative-function routes — the obstruction shown to be evadable in some cases by using linear forms that are constant multiples of one another, with instances recorded. Correlation conjectures — modified forms of the multiplicative-correlation conjectures formulated, with averaged and logarithmically averaged versions proved. Conditional routes through irregular zeros — a recorded result that a strong violation of the underlying randomness principle would imply the conjecture. The convergence of the sum of reciprocals of twin primes — consistent with finitely or infinitely many. Random models of the primes — predict the density accurately and are models.

**Narrowing recorded.** Bounded-gap results narrow the relation: infinitude stands proved for some gap at most 246 in place of the gap of two.

**Progress.** The bounded-gap results and their stated floor. The proved parity obstruction. The convergence result. The measured agreement of the conjectured density with computation.

**Own record.**


## 177 The fold as a kind

**Conditions prior.** The statement holds across readings. The terms have determinate reference. The accounts hold their truth value while tested. A procedure repeated returns the same outcome independently of what precedes it. Nothing prior enters.

**Statement.** Roughly a thousand distinct folds account for most known protein structures, and unrelated sequences converge on the same fold. What makes two structures the same fold?

**The field's account of its hardness.** That the classifications disagree with each other on the assignments they make, that the disagreement concentrates at the boundaries between folds rather than at their centres, and that the two long-standing databases are built on different criteria — one on evolutionary relationship read from structure, one on structural comparison — with no procedure settling a case where they differ.

**Accounts.** Folds are discrete kinds: structures cluster, the clusters recur across unrelated sequences, and the recurrence is what a fold names.

Folds are regions of a continuum: structure space is continuous, the clusters are dense regions in it, and the boundaries are drawn by the classifier's own thresholds.

**Specifications.** A fold is an equivalence class of structures under a similarity relation — universal under structural classification. Structures are comparable by a metric on their coordinates — universal under structural alignment.

**Specifications withdrawn.**

**Common requirement.** That there is a similarity relation on structures under which a fold is well-defined, the dispute concerning where its boundary falls.

**Conserving relation.** Two structures assigned to the same fold, the assignment standing independently of which classification makes it.

**Conserving reach.** All pairs of structures, boundary cases included.

**Reach.** The statement quantifies over the fold assignment of an arbitrary structure pair. Assignments made by particular classifications under particular criteria are what is measured. The two are not the same.

**Unreachability.** Structural alignment scores are continuous and the assignment is a cut on them, the cut declared and not measured; classifications built on different criteria differ at the boundary cases and agree at the centres; no procedure settles a case where they differ without adopting one classification's criterion as the standard.

**Persistence.** The fold assignment standing for an arbitrary structure pair, boundary cases included.

**Addresses.** The classifications and their criteria: Murzin et al., "SCOP: a structural classification of proteins database", Journal of Molecular Biology 247:536-540, 1995; Orengo et al., "CATH — a hierarchic classification of protein domain structures", Structure 5:1093-1108, 1997. The disagreement and the continuity: Sadowski and Taylor, "Are proteins in structure space?", Journal of Molecular Biology, 2010; Kolodny, Petrey and Honig, "Protein structure comparison: implications for the nature of fold space", Current Opinion in Structural Biology 16:393-398, 2006. Remaining properties: keyed at the next pass.

*The report.*

**Approaches.** Evolutionary classification — assigns by relationship inferred from structure, the inference disputed at the boundary. Geometric classification — assigns by alignment score against a threshold, the threshold declared. Structure-space embedding — places structures in a continuous space and reports that the clusters have no sharp boundaries. Sequence-independent comparison — finds recurrence across unrelated sequences, which every account carries and which settles no assignment.

**Narrowing recorded.** Each classification narrows the reach: the assignment stands under that classification's criterion.

**Progress.** The recurrence, undisputed. The count of clusters at any chosen threshold. The agreement at cluster centres.

**Own record.**

## 178 The single native state

**Conditions prior.** The statement holds across readings. The terms have determinate reference. The accounts hold their truth value while tested. A procedure repeated returns the same outcome independently of what precedes it. Nothing prior enters.

**Statement.** A protein is said to fold to one native structure determined by its sequence. What is the native state of a sequence whose measured structure is an ensemble?

**The field's account of its hardness.** That a substantial fraction of eukaryotic proteins carry regions that fold to no single structure and are functional in that condition; that a crystallographic or cryo-EM model is a single set of coordinates fitted to data averaged over many molecules; and that many models can fit one dataset equally well, so the reported structure is one of a set the measurement does not distinguish among.

**Accounts.** The native state is one structure, and ensembles are the exception: most proteins fold to a defined structure, and the disordered ones are a distinct class.

The native state is an ensemble, and one structure is the exception: every protein occupies a distribution of conformations, and a single reported structure is the distribution's most populated member or its average.

**Specifications.** A protein has a native state which is the global minimum of its free energy — universal under the thermodynamic hypothesis. A structure is a set of atomic coordinates — universal under structural biology.

**Specifications withdrawn.**

**Common requirement.** That there is a native state a sequence determines, the dispute concerning whether it is one conformation or a distribution.

**Conserving relation.** The native state determined for a sequence, the determination standing independently of the measurement that reports it.

**Conserving reach.** All sequences, disordered and ensemble-carrying sequences included.

**Reach.** The statement quantifies over the native state of an arbitrary sequence. Ensemble-averaged measurements and single fitted models are what is measured. The two are not the same.

**Unreachability.** A crystallographic model is fitted to data averaged over many molecules and many models fit one dataset equally well; ensemble-refinement methods return distributions rather than resolving among them; disordered regions return no single structure at any resolution reached; a prediction method trained on single deposited structures returns single structures by construction and is not measured against ensembles.

**Persistence.** The native state determined for an arbitrary sequence, ensemble-carrying sequences included.

**Addresses.** Model degeneracy against one dataset and ensemble refinement: Burnley et al., "Modelling dynamics in protein crystal structures by ensemble refinement", eLife 1:e00311, 2012. Disorder as functional and widespread: Wright and Dyson, "Intrinsically disordered proteins in cellular signalling and regulation", Nature Reviews Molecular Cell Biology 16:18-29, 2015. Remaining properties: keyed at the next pass.

*The report.*

**Approaches.** Crystallography — returns one model fitted to averaged data, the averaging stated in the method. Ensemble refinement — returns a distribution fitting the same data, and the distribution is not unique. NMR — returns a set of models consistent with restraints, the set reported rather than resolved. Single-particle cryo-EM — classifies particles into states, the classification count declared. Learned prediction — returns one structure per sequence, trained on single deposited structures.

**Narrowing recorded.** Ensemble refinement narrows the relation: a distribution consistent with the data stands, and which distribution does not.

**Progress.** The structures reported across the observed domain. The measured degeneracy of models against one dataset. The measured extent of disorder across proteomes.

**Own record.**

## 179 The explanatory gap

**Conditions prior.** The statement holds across readings. The terms have determinate reference. The accounts hold their truth value while tested. A procedure repeated returns the same outcome independently of what precedes it. Nothing prior enters.

**Statement.** A physical account of a process states which physical facts obtain, and it leaves open why those facts are accompanied by experience of the character they are accompanied by. Is the gap in the explanation, or in what the explanation is about?

**The field's account of its hardness.** Stated as a gap in explanation and marked as not by itself a claim about what exists: an identity of the kind offered leaves an explanatory residue where identities elsewhere leave none, and the residue is that nothing in the account says why the state feels as it does rather than otherwise or at all.

**Accounts.** The gap is epistemic: the facts are physical and an account of them will close the gap, or the gap follows from how the physical is represented and not from what obtains.

The gap is ontological: an explanatory residue standing where identities elsewhere leave none is evidence that the facts are not exhausted by the physical.

The gap is permanent and neither: the terms available to a self do not couple in the way an explanation of this kind would require, and no account either closes it or licenses a conclusion about what exists.

**Specifications.** An adequate explanation of an identity leaves no residue — local. The residue is a property of the explanation and not of the explainer — local.

**Specifications withdrawn.**

**Common requirement.** That an explanatory residue either does or does not bear on what exists.

**Conserving relation.** A determination of whether the residue bears on what exists, standing on what an explanation delivers.

**Conserving reach.** All accounts of a physical process together with an experiential character.

**Reach.** The statement quantifies over what an explanation delivers. Explanations given, and reports of what they leave open, are what is measured. The two are not the same.

**Unreachability.** A residue read at an explanation is read by a self carrying the concepts the explanation is given in, and no procedure separates a residue in the account from a residue in the reading. The determination is not reached over all accounts by explanations given.

**Persistence.** An account of a physical process from which the experiential character follows in the way the character of water follows from its account, agreed by both.

**Addresses.** The term and the epistemic framing: Levine, "Materialism and Qualia: The Explanatory Gap", Pacific Philosophical Quarterly 64:354-361, 1983. Its standing beside the conceivability argument: keyed at entry 111's addresses. Remaining properties: keyed at the next pass.

*The report.*

**Approaches.** Closing by further physical account — the gap held to narrow as the functions are explained, with the residue reported unchanged at each closing. The phenomenal concept strategy — the residue accounted for by the character of the concepts an explanation is read in, shared with entry 111 and carrying that entry's stated dilemma. Denying the residue — the further question held not to stand once the functions are explained, defended and a minority position. Holding the gap permanent — the residue held to follow from the concepts available and to license no conclusion about what exists.

**Narrowing recorded.** The phenomenal concept strategy weakens the relation: the residue stands accounted for at the reading rather than determined at the account.

**Progress.** The residue reported unchanged across four decades of further physical account. The parting of the epistemic from the ontological claim, stated at the term's own introduction.

**Own record.** The gap is stated as a gap in explanation, and the step from it to a claim about what exists is a separate step the term itself does not take.


## 180 The universal totality

**Conditions prior.** The statement holds across readings. The terms have determinate reference. The accounts hold their truth value while tested. A procedure repeated returns the same outcome. Nothing prior enters.

**Statement.** For any property there is the set of exactly the things having it; in particular, there is a set of all sets. Does the set of all sets that are not members of themselves contain itself?

**The field's account of its hardness.** The totality is both demanded and forbidden: every account gathering all sets yields a set the gathering does not contain, and the concept extends past each totality proposed for it. The field's founder himself parted multiplicities into those that can be thought of as one finished thing and those that cannot, placing the totality of all sets in the second — the account of the hardness arriving from the account's own author.

**Accounts.** Comprehension restricted: sets arise only by separation from given sets, and no universal set exists. The universal set retained, with the comprehension or the logic weakened to hold it. The totality standing as a proper class, gatherable and a member of nothing. The concept indefinitely extensible: every proposed totality of sets is exceeded by a set the proposal itself defines.

**Specifications.** Every determinate property collects a set — universal under naive comprehension. Membership divides cases — universal under classical semantics.

**Specifications withdrawn.** Unrestricted comprehension, withdrawn with the accounts retained, the refutation identifying no member of the five.

**Common requirement.** That all sets can be gathered and quantified over as one completed reach.

**Conserving relation.** Membership divides cases, a thing in a set or not, the division standing without exception.

**Conserving reach.** All sets, the gathering itself included.

**Reach.** The statement quantifies over absolutely all of the kind. The theories' quantifiers range over domains each of which is again a totality the next quantification exceeds. The two are not the same, and the accounts state the parting themselves.

**Unreachability.** The comprehension instance for non-self-membership yields a member exactly when it is not one. The power of any totality strictly exceeds it, and no greatest totality stands. The division is not preserved over the full reach by any account retaining both: each repair on record narrows the reach, weakens the division, or places the totality outside the membership relation it was to close under.

**Persistence.** Exhibition of a completed totality of all sets, closed under its own gathering, with membership classical across it — or a demonstration that quantification over all sets requires none.

**Addresses.** Statement, hardness, accounts: Irvine and Deutsch, "Russell's Paradox", Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy; the founder's parting of multiplicities as the field's surveys report his late correspondence.

*The report.*

**Approaches.** Separation and foundation — the reach narrowed to subsets of given sets, the standard resolution and the standard theory. Type stratification — the division kept by ranking the reach. The universal set retained under stratified comprehension. Proper classes — the totality moved out of the membership relation's range. Indefinite extensibility — the reach denied completion by the concept's own extending. Each approach is placed by the field against the four broad response types available to any paradox of this form.

**Narrowing recorded.** Separation narrows the reach to what given sets already contain. Class theory moves the totality outside the relation. Extensibility denies the reach a last.

**Progress.** The failure carries at theorem grade, the accounts themselves stating it. Every consistent repair on record is recorded as reach-narrowing or division-relocation, and the recurrence of the totality question at each repair — the class of all classes, the domain of all domains — is stated in the field's own surveys.

**Own record.** The argument contains no material of any kind: sets are incidental, and the same argument runs at any totality gathered under its own kind — the catalogue of all catalogues, the word for the words not naming themselves.

## The collection

The entries stand here, one to a problem, **and their count is recomputed at them** — a count carried beside them ages the moment one arrives.

---

# 6 Checks

**C1.** A property without an address.

**C2.** An approach in the published literature, unlisted, or listed with an ending other than where the literature has it.

**C3.** A statement other than the canonical one, or a variant omitted.

**C4.** A specification the statement carries, unlisted.

**C5.** A condition prior stated other than as the five stand.

**C6.** A property stating more than its address carries.

**C7.** An approach's ending stated more strongly or more weakly than the record supports.

**C8.** An address keyed from no property.

**C9.** A property keyed to an address not carrying it.

**C10.** A date or a person functioning as attribution in a property.

**C11.** An unreachability stated where the result reports an attempt rather than a failure of the relation over the reach.

**C12.** A conserving relation or conserving reach stated other than as the problem states it.

**C13.** An account stating the condition of the effort rather than proposing an account.

**C14.** A specification recorded as withdrawn, not carried at the property for it.

**C15.** A narrowing in the record, not carried at the property for it.

**C16.** A property carrying a comparison with another entry, or a relation between accounts.

**C17.** A field's statement of its own problem's hardness, standing in its literature, not carried at the property for it.

**C18.** A hardness line stating the compendium's reading rather than the field's words.

**C19.** An approach count matching the count at neighbouring entries where the fields' records differ.

Corrections are applied against the literature.

---

# 7 The aggregate

Each figure is a count over filled properties.

The count of entries. The count of entries by the name their specifications are universal under. The distribution of specifications carried per entry. The count of entries carrying an unreachability result. The count carrying a narrowing recorded, by narrowing of reach and by weakening of relation. The count carrying a specification withdrawn. The distribution of approaches per entry and of approach standings. The count whose settling result is specified as one result, as either of two, or as proved unavailable. The count of properties standing empty, by property.

Each figure is recomputable from the entries.
